# SPEC — the single definition of "correct" (alignment round, 2026-10-13)

Everything the workbook shows is built and tested against this file. A figure that does not match its row here is a
bug; a figure with no row here is a gap in this file. Written before the code changes of the alignment round (Jake's
"FULL ALIGNMENT ROUND", 2026-10-13); the Coverage tab and `coverage_audit.csv` check the built workbook against it.

**Status legend.** `BUILT` = built and matches this row · `FIX` = built, wrong, fixed this round (the item named) ·
`NEW` = built this round · `NOT WIRED` = no route wired yet (the route is named) · `LATER` = deliberately not this
round. **PROPOSED** marks a definition or mapping that is Jake's to confirm; the conservative reading is built and the
cell note says PROPOSED.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
## 1. Rules the code must follow

1. **ONE COMPUTATION PER CONCEPT.** Float, lock rate, stake inside circulating, emissions, unlocks, issuance, burn,
   net issuance, HVY, NHY, FDV and dilution are computed ONCE (a read-time view or one shared formula builder in
   `build_workbook.py`) and every tab READS that value. No tab-local versions. The cross-tab test fails the build when
   a concept shown on more than one tab differs between tabs.
2. **NO UNEXPLAINED BLANKS.** Every headline cell is one of: a value · `n/a — <reason>` (the quantity does not exist
   for this project) · `0 — <reason>` (it exists and is genuinely zero: a programme that ended, a fixed supply that
   mints nothing) · `BLOCKED — <reason>` (it exists but the inputs cannot support it yet — unequal coverage, a series
   break) · `NOT WIRED — <route>` (it exists, no source is wired; the route is named). A bare `n/a` is a bug.
3. **0 IS NOT n/a.** Where the quantity is genuinely zero the cell reads 0 with the reason (Maple emissions after
   MIP-019 / Drips' end, NEAR unlocks where no lockup released, Fluid holder value after the 2026-05-11 halt).
4. **EXPLICIT DIRECTION ON EVERY ROW** (+1 higher is better · −1 lower is better · 0 neutral). The Trajectory never
   prints "direction not declared".
5. **AXIS ON EVERY ROW.** BUSINESS · CAPTURE · ALIGNMENT · CONTEXT (section 2). Shown as a column on CP and Trajectory.
6. **RANGES THAT FAIL THE BUILD.** Lock rate and float ÷ circulating in 0..1; float ≥ 0; whole-token yield ≤ staker
   yield; releases ≥ 0; issuance ≥ 0. A flow statement whose residual exceeds 0.5% carries a CHECK note with its named cause, or is BROKEN (FR2 T1).
7. **NET ISSUANCE = MINT − BURN.** A pre-minted supply mints nothing: its issuer RELEASES are shown on their own line
   and are never inside net issuance (Total Yield subtracts them explicitly).
8. **STAKE INSIDE CIRCULATING.** Every float, lock rate and whole-token yield reads the stake LESS the stake held by
   wallets already outside circulating (`_stake_inside` / `_ff_lock`; Hyperliquid's team, Foundation and Grants stake).
9. **COVERAGE.** A flow annualised over the days it covered; two sides of a net or ratio over materially different
   spans are BLOCKED (`_coverage_guard same_span`). A series whose older leg stops short of its current leg has a hole
   between them, not zeros (coverage counts from the current leg).
10. **BASIS.** A change of measuring point is a SERIES BREAK on the Trajectory unless declared as a handover; history is
    restated on the current basis where a route exists (section 7). A provider's reclassification is a break
    (`config.SOURCE_RECLASSIFICATIONS`).
11. **MODELLED legs** are labelled, never inside Q0, excluded from scoring.
12. **SOURCES.** Primary first (protocol docs, contracts, governance, on-chain); a dashboard source says so on its row
    with what an on-chain replacement needs. Nothing filled from inference.
13. **NEVER WRITE A FLOW OFF BECAUSE THE CHAIN CAN'T TRACE IT** (Jake, 2026-10-20, G9 N). "The on-chain trace is the
    priority, but it is not the only evidence. If the chain can't trace a flow, that means we can't trace it, not that it
    doesn't happen." Every cell takes the highest step available and carries it:
    1. `ON-CHAIN` — a complete on-chain measurement;
    2. `PUBLISHED: <source>` — the project's own published figure (dashboard, report, governance post, official
       announcement), cited and dated;
    3. `AGGREGATOR: <source>` — DefiLlama, Token Terminal, Artemis free pages, growthepie, Blockworks, cited;
    4. `MECHANISM × PUBLISHED BASE` — a declared mechanism applied to a published base (GEODNET: 80% of revenue to
       buy-and-burn).
    A partial on-chain trace is the CHECK on a step-2/3/4 value (a lower bound or a reconciliation); it never replaces
    the value with 0 or a blank. The cell is VERIFIED when the chain check agrees within the line's tolerance,
    LIVE-UNVERIFIED otherwise, and CHECK (both figures shown) when the check disagrees by more than the tolerance.
    "0 — no such flow" needs POSITIVE evidence that the mechanism does not exist (docs, contract code, governance); not
    finding it on-chain is not evidence. Class E ("no source") is allowed only after steps 2–4 were checked, and the
    cell lists the sources checked, with dates (`config.FLOW_LINE_EVIDENCE` field `checked`; tests/test_round_g9n.py).

## 2. Axes (mapping; PROPOSED where marked)

| Axis | Holds |
|---|---|
| BUSINESS | usage, customers, fees, revenue, volume, TVL, market share, Business 1–4, fees ÷ issuance (PROPOSED) |
| CAPTURE | holder value yield, net holder yield (PROPOSED), payout ratios, buyback / burn, retirement, protocol yield, validator yield and Total Yield (PROPOSED), capture status, switch power |
| ALIGNMENT | dilution (a–e), unlocks, gross / net issuance, net supply change, discretionary supply, insider share, subsidy cover, supplier payout, leakage, uniformity, lock terms, destination durability |
| CONTEXT | price, market cap, FDV, circulating, float, lock rate (PROPOSED), valuation multiples (NRR, free float ÷ ARR, fees ÷ FDV — PROPOSED), T-bill, SSR, ETF / DAT holdings |

## 3. Trajectory methods

* `4Q log-linear` — five consecutive 90-day blocks (Q0, Q-1..Q-4), the row's own formula each; growth = log-linear
  slope over the quarters on one basis.
* `rolling T12M` — a discrete / lumpy flow (unlocks, buybacks with a cadence, monthly series): direction and growth
  from Q0..Q-3 vs Q-1..Q-4 sums.
* `stock-now` — a stock: Q0 = the latest value, earlier quarters = each quarter's mean; growth log-linear on those.

## 4. Concept registry (machine-readable — parsed by `spec.py`)

Columns: key | concept | definition | formula | unit | axis | dir | trajectory | tabs | tier | status | inputs.
`dir` is +1 / −1 / 0. `tier` is the best source tier the concept reads (1 aggregator/API, 2 on-chain, 3 page /
manual). `inputs` are the config.METRICS series the concept may read (the Coverage audit's MATCHES-SPEC test).

## 8. Flow statements (C11) — tab "Flows", Q0..Q-4 per project

* **Token flow (as built, FR2 F1/T1):** opening circulating + mint (the issuance basis; 0 on a pre-minted token) +
  releases (pool_release_tokens on a pre-minted token, else the non-circulating wallets' falls; GEODNET: the mining
  release AND the other wallets' release, disjoint; where circulating IS a published schedule — Aethir — the schedule's
  own monthly step, "+ scheduled releases") − returned to issuer-side wallets (the rises of the same stock: recalls,
  purchases into a Safe; a mint into the set is NOT a return) − burns = expected closing. A memo line shows mints
  straight into issuer-side wallets (not circulating until released). Residual = closing − expected; |residual| > 0.5%
  of opening with a NAMED cause (a leg short of the quarter, a basis change, a third party's circulating figure, a
  declared unwired flow) → CHECK; with NO named cause → BROKEN on the Coverage tab.
* **Value flow:** fees → supplier share (fees − revenue) + protocol revenue; revenue → retired (burned + held
  buybacks) + distributed (to holders) + treasury (the rest); incentives $ (outflows a–e) beside. Residual = revenue −
  retired − distributed − treasury (0 by construction for treasury as the remainder; shown so a negative treasury —
  value paid out beyond revenue — is visible).

## 9. Leakage and uniformity (C12 — PROPOSED definitions)

* Leakage (i): share of distributed holder value received by insider / issuer-side wallets (their share of the
  reward-bearing stake). (ii): incentive spend not returned as revenue = max(0, 1 − subsidy cover). (iii): buyback
  execution = $ spent ÷ (tokens bought × the day's market price) − 1.
* Uniformity: whole-token yield ÷ staker yield (1 = every holder treated alike); non-stakers receive value: yes / no.

## 11. LATER (C19) — Proven / Building / Fading / Failing grid

Inputs it will need, all already on the workbook after this round: per row its axis (section 2), level (Q0 value) and
trajectory (growth, direction, quarters improved). Nothing built.

### Scoring principles register (LATER, not scored) — Jake, from the Aerodrome call (G6 brief 2026-10-19)

Kept as principles only; no column scores them.

1. Pay your most important external user (on a DEX, the LP).
2. When code replaces most of the team, return as close to 100% of value as possible.
3. LPs have no network-effect lock-in.
4. A dollar of buyback isn't a dollar of value accrual unless credibly permanent.
5. Distribute revenue directly, and the token needs a function beyond being bought back.
6. Fees taken from LPs/users to fund a team invite a fork.
7. Long locks are a feature.
8. The moat is a network of owners.
9. Holders should direct emissions to activity with attributable revenue.
10. Partnering is easiest between protocols with the same economics.

## 12. PROPOSED — for Jake (Jake's decisions of 2026-10-13 recorded under each; FR2 = fix round 2)

1. Axis of lock rate, valuation multiples (NRR, free float ÷ ARR, fees ÷ FDV) → CONTEXT; NHY, validator yield and Total
   Yield → CAPTURE; fees ÷ issuance → BUSINESS.
2. Aerodrome's rebase to veAERO lockers is a demand-side incentive (c), its own line; gauge emissions are (b); no (a).
3. Hyperliquid (a) counts only rewards to stake inside circulating (pro-rata to stake, the protocol's own rule).
4. Leakage (i)–(iii) and uniformity as defined in section 9.
5. Supplier payout = (fees − revenue + supply-side incentive $) ÷ fees.
6. Lock rate direction +1 (more locked inside circulating is better).
7. B4 (Sky) needs no issuance change: gross issuance 0 is verified on-chain. The +151.4M QoQ circulating rise is MKR→SKY
   conversions out of the converter's pre-minted balance. PROPOSED: shown on the Flows tab as a release, and NOT
   counted as dilution in (e). Each conversion retires the equivalent MKR (24,000 SKY per MKR), so holders' claims are
   unchanged.
8. Supplier payout counts the supply-side incentive $ (b) with the fees paid to suppliers; the fee-only ratio is beside
   it (Aerodrome 1.14 / 0.24 on the 2026-10-09 dump).
9. Pendle: the Governance multisig's falls (the incentives reserve) belong in (b), with the days it funds the
   GaugeController taken out (else counted twice with emissions) — needs a per-transfer series; kept in (e) until then.
10. Ether.fi: the Q0 sETHFI top-ups (1,137,473 ETHFI) are TREASURY-funded (FIFO trace: 28% earlier treasury buyback
    stock, 25% Treasury, 24% investor / core-contributor custodian, 23% Binance withdrawal; 0% revenue) — recorded, not
    credited; HVY 0 stands (ASSUMED pending Jake).
11. NEAR revenue = gas revenue + NEAR Intents fee-switch revenue (DefiLlama near-intents dailyRevenue); the payout and
    coverage ratios divide by both.
12. ~~GEODNET discretionary supply = the treasury wallets only~~ — REJECTED by Jake (addendum G2): every non-mining
    issuer wallet (Team, Investor, Ecosystem, Vendor/Marketing, Public sale — 20M unsold public-sale Safe included) is
    discretionary; the mining wallets are (b) only. No dated release schedule exists (filing §8/§15 give durations
    without start dates — research 2026-10-13).
13. Hyperliquid insider share of stake = the stake of every wallet outside circulating (team + Foundation + Grants) ÷
    total staked, not the team alone.
14. Divergence: a revenue-vs-price growth gap within ±10pp reads "in line" (B14) — ACCEPTED as a convention; each
    Trajectory row now shows the standard error of its 4Q slope (annual, delta method) so the band can be calibrated. Growth: a quarter outside 10%–10x of
    Q0 ends the regime in either direction (B13).
15. Uniswap (d) = every fall of the governance Timelock (the growth budget and any other grant), the treasury-funded
    100M burn excepted.

## 19. ROUND G (2026-10-18) — amendments, old → new

Phase 1 — run reliability.

| # | Item | Old | New |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1.1a | Concurrent connections | No limit. `fetch._dispatch` started ~45 tiers in the same instant. Every `Http` owned a `requests.Session`. `ChainReader` built a new web3 `HTTPProvider` (new session, new TLS handshake) for most past-state reads. Jake's full runs failed 27–60 calls (SSLError / ConnectionError across ~25 hosts, 16–22 s each, mostly in the first 60–90 s). The same projects in four groups failed 4. | Under every requests call (web3 included), `fetch.base._instrumented_send` holds a run-wide slot (`TOKEN_METRICS_MAX_CONN`, 16) and a per-host slot (`TOKEN_METRICS_MAX_CONN_PER_HOST`, 4), host first. Calls waiting are registered as "queued for <host>". Calls go through ONE shared connection pool (`_SHARED_ADAPTER`) unless the caller configured its own adapter. Headers, auth and cookies stay per session; only sockets are shared. Run summary line: "connections: at most N in flight … M call(s) queued …". |
| G1.1e | The send patch's module | `fetch.base` installed `_instrumented_send` once. `import config` runs `validate_config()`, which reached `fetch.validate` → `fetch.base` before config was complete. That `fetch.base` then failed (`config.TRAJECTORY` not yet defined), but its patch stayed installed, so every HTTP call ran on an orphaned module. Its in-flight registry was not the one the heartbeat read, which is why timeouts said "waiting on: nothing registered". Confirmed at bf53e87: `HTTPAdapter.send.__globals__ is vars(fetch.base)` → False. | `config._relation_pairs` does not import fetch while config is still loading (`_CONFIG_LOADED`, the last line of config.py; the answer at import was [] before too). The live `fetch.base` always (re)installs its send, wrapping requests' true original (`_tm_original`). |
| G1.1b | Connect errors | Retried only by `Http` (backoff 2, 4, 8 … s). web3 and raw `requests` calls failed on the first SSLError. | A connect-phase failure (SSLError, or a ConnectionError that is neither a timeout nor a ProxyError) is retried `TOKEN_METRICS_CONNECT_RETRIES` (2) times with jittered backoff (2 s, 4 s), and each retry is logged with the host. Inside `Http`, its own loop retries instead, so retries are never doubled. |
| G1.1c | Tier starts | All at once. | Each tier starts `TOKEN_METRICS_TIER_STAGGER` (0.5 s) after the previous one. Its budget counts from its own start. |
| G1.1d | robots.txt unreachable | After 3 attempts: complete disallow (RFC 9309 §2.3.1.4), re-checked once at the end of the run. | Every definite answer (2xx file, 4xx) is cached in `<logcache>/robots-cache.json`. When the file is unreachable, the copy read within 30 days is used (RFC 9309 §2.4): its Disallow rules still block, and a cached 404 still allows. With no copy: complete disallow, as before. |
| G1.2a | Past state on a chain with no archive | Each past-block read tried every endpoint again. Jake's Ether.fi run paid ~13–25 s per read for 13 reads. | `ChainReader._past_state`: once every endpoint has refused for a reason that is not about one block (403, no archive state), the chain is marked NO HISTORICAL STATE for the run, and later past-block reads fail at once with that reason. |
| G1.2b | Empty eth_call return (`b''`) | Treated as an endpoint refusal. | The same endpoint is asked for `eth_getCode` at that block. No code means the contract was NOT YET DEPLOYED, so the balance or supply is 0 and logged as such. Example: ETHFI on Optimism 0xe0080d2F… was deployed at block 149,604,348 (2026-03-29 18:04 UTC); mainnet.optimism.io, drpc and tenderly agree. |
| G1.2c | Optimism archive endpoint | Public endpoints only (publicnode 403; mainnet.optimism.io and drpc empty before deployment; nodies and tenderly "block not found"). | `fetch.chain.alchemy_url`: a chain with no `<CHAIN>_RPC_URL` uses the Alchemy key already in another `<CHAIN>_RPC_URL`, on that chain's Alchemy host (opt-mainnet, polygon-mainnet, bnb-mainnet, berachain-mainnet, …). A network the app has not enabled answers 403, which marks the endpoint down. `TOKEN_METRICS_NO_ALCHEMY_DERIVE=1` turns this off. |
| G1.2d | Abandoned tiers | Marked TIMED OUT while the thread ran on. The OP probe logged after the run summary, and aero_voter's marker was written after its frames had been dropped (G1.3). | `_abandon` abandons the thread. Its next HTTP call or registered sleep raises `TierAbandoned`, and its log lines are dropped (`DROP_ABANDONED` on every handler). Pool workers inherit their tier through `pool_owner()`. |
| G1.2e | Explorer-hole bisection | Used balanceOf(latest) minus the explorer's logged tail when archive state failed. Built from the same explorer logs being checked, it differs by the same amount at every block before the hole, so the bisection always went left (79,180,853 → 19,331). | The hole bisection uses archive state only, and with none it stops at once and says why. A window reconciliation's OPENING balance must also be a real past-state read when the window's logs came from an explorer; tip-minus-tail at both ends cannot fail. The full-history reconciliation keeps the tail route, which is a genuine check (live balance vs every logged transfer). |
| G1.2f | Ether.fi cashback holder | Sourced as "cash-v3 deployments (alignment round C1)". | Re-confirmed at etherfi-protocol/cash-v3@a5024d0b `deployments/mainnet/10/deployments.json`: "CashbackDistributor": 0x38F2fBb2…. Its source is "On-chain settlement gate for ETHFI cashback claims". On OP, the CashbackDispatcher 0xef55eC69… whitelists ETHFI but has never transferred it (explorer.optimism.io token-transfers, read 2026-10-18; it pays USDC). The Distributor's outflow is the whole ETHFI cashback on OP. |
| G1.3 | aero_voter epoch marker | A date. Set by the reading thread even after the tier was abandoned, so 2026-10-15's runs said "marked read but its rows are not all stored — reading it again" (~100 s, past 180 s) until 10:47. | "<date>\|<metrics produced>", set only after the rows are emitted and the tier is not abandoned. The store decides: produced rows that are not stored are read again. A metric a complete read did NOT produce (no split, no illiquid figure) is named once and not re-read for. |
| G1.4 | ve_managed | Buyback window (archive Safe-nonce bisection) ran first. Its retry naps were bare `time.sleep`, unregistered ("waiting on: nothing registered") and blind to the budget. | Holdings and the lock stock run first, the window last. `check_offline_items._retry` / `_rpc_retry` nap through `fetch.base.sleep` (registered, abandonable, naming the error). The window runs under `call_budget(WINDOW_BUDGET_S = 80)`; past that it stops with the reason, stores nothing, and is read again next run. |
| G1.5 | Plasma past-state read | `self._w3_by_url[url] = w3` raised AttributeError when the kept endpoint answered first. | `_w3_by_url` is created in `__init__` and assigned through `setdefault`. Every past-state read goes through `_past_state`, with one Web3 per endpoint for the run. |
| G1.6 | HTTP 403 from an endpoint | Next endpoint for that call only; every later call paid the 403 again. Log reads began on the kept endpoint even when it was a public one. | A 403 marks the endpoint DOWN for the run, and every later read on the chain skips it. Log reads and past-state reads start on the first endpoint in `rpc_endpoints` order (keyed first) that is not down. `web3()` logs which endpoints failed before the kept one. |
| G1.7a | NEAR BigQuery login | Banner at the end of the run only. | The Run Log opens with an ACTION NEEDED block (`build_workbook.run_log_actions`) carrying the two gcloud commands. While the latest attempt needed a login, the Gap Report lists "[open] NEAR BigQuery login" at P2, with the lapse dates (`near_bigquery.login_state`). |
| G1.7b | adc_check.py | Ran in-process. google-auth's reauth asks for a password on the terminal, and under the daily runner the prompt is captured, so run_2026-10-18.log stopped at "=== BigQuery check". | The check runs in a child process with no stdin (the interactive challenge is refused, exit 2) and a 90 s timeout (`TOKEN_METRICS_ADC_TIMEOUT`, exit 9). daily_run.ps1 waits at most `-LoginWaitMinutes` (5) for an interactive login, then carries on with NEAR BigQuery skipped. |
| G1.7c | Missed days after a re-login | Top-up: every missing day newer than the newest held, up to 31 per run. | Unchanged (it is already idempotent: a lapse of N ≤ 31 days is filled by the first good run). A test now pins it. |
| G1.8 | Two runs on one metrics.db | Nothing prevented it (daily run 10:42, manual runs 10:48). | `runlock.RunLock`: an OS lock on `<metrics.db>.lock`, released by the OS when the process ends. The holder is named in `.lock.who`. A second token_metrics.py (run, seed or --no-fetch) waits up to `TOKEN_METRICS_LOCK_WAIT` (1800 s), saying so every minute, then exits 75 with a plain message. daily_run.ps1 logs "daily run FINAL: complete …" or, from its `finally`, "daily run FINAL: stopped early – steps that ran: …". |
| G1.9 | `--project X` workbook | Drawn at the fetched scope ("1 project(s) rendered of 30"). | `--project X` fetches X only. The workbook draws the run's default scope (portfolio.txt, or --all) from the store, plus X if it is outside that scope (`render_scope`). `--no-fetch --project X` does the same. |
| G1.10 | Budgets (hl_candles 600 s, boros 480 s) | — | Unchanged. They are re-measured on Jake's next full run under the caps; the connections line says how much queueing there was. |
| G1.11 | Logging stream | `logging.basicConfig` wrote to stderr, which under PowerShell 5.1 `2>&1` turns every INFO line into a NativeCommandError record. | token_metrics.py and archive_backfill.py log to stdout. |

Phase 2 — data correctness.

| # | Item | Old | New |
|---|---|---|---|
| G2.1 | Prices before CoinGecko's free year | `price_usd` started where CoinGecko's free market_chart stops (365 days). Ether.fi: no price before 2025-09-12, so 433 of 834 buyback days were unvalued from 2024-07-06. | New tier `price_history` (tier 1, 120 s, once a day per project). Every day from the project's first TOKEN FLOW (any stored series of kind flow, unit tokens) to yesterday that has no `price_usd` is filled from DefiLlama's `coins.llama.fi/chart/coingecko:<id>` (CoinGecko's price relayed, 00:00 UTC points within 10 min; ≤ 499 points a call; robots.txt 404 = no rules). Source `price_history:coins.llama.fi[coingecko:<id>]`. A stored price is never replaced, a past flow is still never valued at today's price, and the change check skips these past-day backfills (not comparable with the latest value). DefiLlama's first point per portfolio token (read 2026-10-18): ETHFI 2024-03-18; SYRUP 2024-11-13; MORPHO 2024-11-20; HYPE 2024-11-29; ATH 2024-06-12; PLUME 2025-01-21; the rest earlier. |
| G2.2a | FDV history | FDV = price × total_supply per day, with `total_supply` forward-filled. On 7 projects that total is CoinGecko's snapshot (no history; first stored 2026-09-11 in Jake's 10-13 dumps), so FDV and every FDV-dependent Trajectory column had ~1 quarter. | `config.FDV_TOTAL_HISTORY`: for the days BEFORE the primary total's first row only, FDV uses the same quantity measured another way. Uniswap, GEODNET: totalSupply − burn balances. Plume: Ethereum totalSupply. Near: block-header total_supply. Ethereum: ultrasound supply-over-time. It is used only while the same-quantity check holds: on the most recent `FDV_HISTORY_MIN_OVERLAP` (14) common days, median \|alt/total − 1\| ≤ `FDV_HISTORY_TOL` (0.5%), checked every build; otherwise nothing is used and the note says why. The basis bracket stays the primary's (same quantity, checked), so no series break; the source names the history leg. Jake's 10-13 dumps: Uniswap, Plume, Near and Ethereum go from 1 to 5 quarters. GEODNET stays at 1: CoinGecko's own total became net of burn on 2026-10-12 (−3.0%), so it extends once 14 days agree, around 2026-10-25. Hyperliquid: FORWARD-ONLY. World Mobile: SOURCE-LIMITED (`config.FDV_SHORT_REASON`). |
| G2.2b | Short history rows | "Why n/a" gave growth reasons only. | Every Trajectory row with fewer than 5 quarters names its youngest input and its kind: `HISTORY FORWARD-ONLY` (the source keeps none), `SOURCE-LIMITED` (no route), or `BUG (a route exists and has not filled it; cost …)` with the fix. The classifier is `history_gaps.fix_for`, the same one logs/history_gaps.csv uses. The coverage audit carries the HISTORY reason for 1–4-quarter rows. |
| G2.2c | Flows "Status" history depth | Read 0 on all 16 projects. | By design: the Status line is a text verdict per quarter, not a series. Its depth is now the number of quarters carrying a verdict other than n/a. |
| G2.6a | `--seed nearblocks` message | "no NEAR account flows configured" while storing 2,416 rows: the line listed only `near_account_flows`, which were retired 2026-10-07. | The BEFORE/AFTER line names what the seed reads: the NearBlocks series and the wallets' balance history (stats/balance), each with its newest stored date, and says the account-txns reads are retired. |
| G2.3a | A dated reference against a daily stock (route "last") | The row on or before the reference date plus one day (the sample lag), so the next day's row won when both existed. Jake's 18 Oct workbook compared the Sky Pause Proxy reference of 2026-10-13 (20,140,000) with the 10-14 row (21,528,971: the SKY received in the 25 hours between, ~43K every ~47 min), giving "BROKEN +6.90% … an intraday move". MKR the same: 83,170 (10-14) against 83,465.89. | The row of the reference's own day first; the one-day lag only when that day has no row. On Jake's 10-13 dump: Pause Proxy 20,142,230 (read 16:22 UTC) vs 20,140,000 → +0.011%, VERIFIED. MKR 83,465.892 vs 83,465.89 → VERIFIED. Tolerances unchanged (1%, 0.1%). |
| G2.3b | Sky TMF net revenue, Sep 2026 | BROKEN +5.05%, "the TMF series does not sit on one basis". | Unchanged status, tolerance kept (2%), cause extended with today's read. Block Analitica P&L: September revenue 30,795,767 − expense 15,416,783 = 15,378,984 (ours, to the dollar); revenue_distribution 8,225,945 is not netted. TMF's 14,640,000 is 738,984 lower, and no single September line is that size. Basis NOT reconciled: documented, not adjusted. |

Hyperliquid's docs (hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/hypercore/aligned-quote-assets, AQAv2, read 2026-10-18): reserve yield is "automatically sent to the Assistance Fund 8 days after each interval completes" from "the system interest address `0x50...00 + {token_index}`". The sender in the Fund's ledger is 0x5000000000000000000000000000000000000000 = 0x50…00 + 0, and token index 0 is USDC (spotMeta), the token paid. A new series `aqa_revenue_usd` reads the Fund's ledger for sends from the documented address(es) since activation (2026-08-26). It is added to Hyperliquid's `revenue_usd` and `holders_revenue_usd` (read-time; DefiLlama's own figures stay as `_defillama`). The note closes. |
| G2-D5a | Aerodrome supply after the 22 Oct cutover | Legacy token only (total_supply, the non-circulating set). | Each AERO once, read from the new contracts once aero_migration.json is complete. new AERO pre-mints the whole migration allocation to the migration contract (dromos-labs/metadex-public@0058bd9b V3/src/token/Token.sol L57-58). Old AERO deposited is HELD in the old escrow under the migration veNFT (Migration.sol L156-158, merge L121-123), never burned. burnRemaining() burns the unused NEW reserve (AerodromeMigration.sol L44-46). Jake's formula (new + old − old held) would count the reserve that backs unmigrated old AERO and that old AERO both. So: **total = (new total − reserve) + min(ratio × (old total − old held), reserve)**, **circulating = (new total − reserve − Velodrome allocation still held − the non-circulating wallets' new AERO) + min(ratio × (old circulating − old held), reserve)**. Here reserve = new AERO held by the Aerodrome migration contract, old held = escrow.locked(MIGRATION_TOKEN_ID) + old AERO held by the contract, and ratio = previewConversion(MIGRATION_TOKEN_ID)'s converted ÷ basis (1:1 in AerodromeMigration L141-143; a non-1:1 read is applied). When the reserve is burned the old term falls to 0 by itself. NEEDS JAKE's confirmation of the reserve term. |
| G2-D5b | Wiring | — | aero_v3 writes `total_supply` and `noncirculating_holding_tokens` (= total − circulating) from the cutover day; both join v3_replaces, so legacy rows of those days are dropped. The migration contract is read from new AERO's `MIGRATION()`. Before anything is read, V2_TOKEN(), V2_ESCROW() and V3_TOKEN() must equal the legacy token, the legacy escrow and the file's token, and every address must hold code. An `addresses.migration` in the file must match. Before the cutover nothing changes; placeholders are refused as in G2.10. |

Then 10-14 12:00 83,452.73, 16:00 83,282.77, 10-15 00:00 83,160.82 (−0.3655%): MKR converted to SKY after the read. G2.3a (the reference's own day first) compares the 10-13 row (83,465.892, read 16:22 UTC) → VERIFIED. Tolerance kept at 0.1%; the reference row now names its block. |

PRIMARY for the buyback: ours, the Fund's own HyperCore balance (spotClearinghouseState), an on-chain stock. ASXN counts executed purchases (a cross-check of the same flow). DefiLlama's holders revenue is a revenue model (fees × share), not a buyback measure. With AQAv2 booked (D4) the AQA days align. |
| G2-L3 | GEODNET FDV | FDV history refused: the overlap check against CoinGecko's total failed (CoinGecko became net of burn on 2026-10-12, −3.0% before). | GEODNET's total supply is on-chain first: GEOD.totalSupply() on Polygon − the burn balances (Polygon burn address + Solana burn account), per day, the same view as Hyperliquid's. The stored CoinGecko totals move to `total_supply_coingecko` (run_sql CU). FDV = price × that total over the whole 401-day series, with no overlap check needed (its FDV_TOTAL_HISTORY entry is retired). |

The run logs "ALCHEMY USAGE this run: N request(s), ~CU"; the month's total is kept in .cache/alchemy_usage.json (machine-local, not committed); at 80% of the Free plan's 30M CU a month (alchemy.com/pricing, read 2026-10-18) the run WARNs, at 95% it says so as a FAILED line. A plan refusal, or a hinted range under 2,000 blocks, marks that endpoint "NO getLogs (plan)" for the run (never asked for logs again) and the scan falls through: the explorer route, then the public RPCs. A hinted range of 2,000 or more is used as the chunk at once (no halving). For Ethereum the public log route is mainnet.gateway.tenderly.co (served a 1,000,000-block eth_getLogs, 2026-10-18; publicnode 403, cloudflare max 800, ankr needs a key, drpc 400). Per-chain <CHAIN>_RPC_URL (prepended) and RPC_<CHAIN> (replace) still win; the old lift from a *_RPC_URL stays as the fallback when ALCHEMY_API_KEY is unset. The key is validated at startup (no "/", whitespace, "<" or ">"; 16–64 characters of [A-Za-z0-9_-]); an invalid key stops the run with a message that never contains it. |

G5 seeds: Morpho Base reconciled to the wei, 584 rows (373,345 new events, 754 s); Plume payouts 242,062 events, 470 rows 2025-04-28..2026-10-18 (not balance-reconciled). Morpho: BROKEN 4 → 0, VERIFIED 22 → 27 (subsidy cover 31.95, dilution (b) 0.57%). | G6-0a | Shared explorer tier | Every project's headline scans shared `explorer` (300 s) and `explorer_l2:<chain>`. | Each project has its own tier and budget: `explorer@<Project>` and `explorer_l2:<chain>@<Project>` (config.scan_tier, explorer_scan_tiers; EXPLORER_PROJECT_TIER_BUDGET_S 300; an L2 tier keeps its chain's budget). `explorer_gauge` unchanged. A timeout stamps only its own tier's scans (gaps.served_by by `own_tier`). Scans wired at start-up by the explorer probe (config.wire_probed_scans: Morpho and Fluid on Base) get their project's tier too — before this fix they kept the bare `explorer_l2:<chain>`, which is in no tier and would never have run. |
| G6-0b | balanceOf reconciliation | Every run re-summed every event and rewrote the whole cache; the poison filter was quadratic. | Incremental: `<cache>/reconciled.json` keeps, per `chain:token:holder`, the last reconciled block, net and counts; the net is the stored net + the events after that block (`_sum_after`), with a full re-sum on any mismatch. LogCache appends a delta segment (`<sid>.d<block>.json.gz`) or a marks file instead of rewriting, compacting past 30 deltas. Poison filter indexed. Measured on a synthetic 581,482-event stream: save 21.3 s → 0.00 s; net 0.46 s; poison filter ~260 s (extrapolated) → 4.6 s; load unchanged (13.9–23 s). The RECONCILED line now says "Reconcile Ns, net incremental/full". Before/after on Jake's store: printed by his next run (not measurable here). |
| G6-0c | Alchemy first for the chain tier | Alchemy was put first whenever a key was set. | Documented free-plan throughput: 300 CU/s (alchemy.com/pricing "300 CU/s"; docs/reference/throughput), 30M CU a month — `alchemy_usage.FREE_TIER_CU_PER_S`; a run-wide pacer keeps every Alchemy call under it. Every RPC call is timed by host (ok / timeout / 429 / http / connection — `chain.RPC_TIMES`), saved to .cache/rpc_latency.json (hosts only) and logged. `connect_order` puts the fallback first when the measured Alchemy median is slower than the fallback's (≥ 20 Alchemy calls and ≥ 3 fallback calls; the fallback is sampled with 3 eth_blockNumber calls when it has no timings). The measurement itself comes from Jake's next run: no figure is claimed here. |
| G6-0d | defillama_tvl | 150 s timeout with frames lost. | Stops TVL_STOP_FLOOR_S 40 s before its budget (deadline = budget − 10 s), keeps what it read, saves the next protocol in a cursor (`defillama_tvl:cursor`) and starts there next run. |
| G6-0 done when | — | — | A normal run with 0 FAILED from time budgets: to be confirmed on Jake's next run. |

#### Item 0 — decisions

| # | Item | Old | New |
|---|---|---|---|
| G7-0a | World Mobile total supply | PROPOSED A / B / C (G6-1a). | DECIDED C, with B its own row. B = total_supply_gross (EVM chains + Solana) + wmtx_cardano_supply_tokens → `total_supply_with_cardano_tokens`. C = B + the Ethereum holders' supply awaiting re-issue = Ethereum totalSupply at block 26,016,081 (the block before the first counterfeit mint: block 26,016,082, 2026-09-20 03:38:23 UTC, tx 0xa0c510d2…c245) **1,013,930,149.415209** − re-issue confirmed on Base (**0**), labelled "inferred from chain: pre-exploit Ethereum supply awaiting re-issue"; C is `total_supply` (FDV reads it) from the exploit day; replaced by World Mobile's published snapshot total once one exists (config `total_supply_option_c.snapshot_total`). **Reconciled on chain:** Base 0x3e31 = 161,336,057.257586 (09-14) + 1,109 CCIP mints 10,066,034.984429 − 394 CCIP burns 2,950,344.123859 + 4 direct `mint()` calls by 0x03fb0f5c… 46,227,317.694 (09-22 23,276,831.334; 09-23 17,951,875.36; 10-05 10; 10-07 4,998,601) = 214,679,065.812156, exact; the mint/burn log set rebuilds totalSupply at all 35 day-end blocks. MINTER_ROLE was granted to 0x03fb and revoked minutes after each mint, by EOA 0xf4e55757…. The direct mints are the "planned WMTX minting on Base … part of the recovery process and preparation for centralized exchange trading to resume" (Telegram "World Mobile Updates", 2026-09-22 13:27 UTC — the channel is not confirmed official). They went to ~10 wallets (an exchange-like EOA 0xab782bc7… 13,000,000; a WMTX/WETH pool; two Safes); no claim or distributor contract and no fan-out to holders; the holder portal is not live (posts 10-08, 10-12, 10-16). So the Review Queue's "Base 0x3e31 is flat at 214,679,066 and the re-issue is not on it" is right for the holder re-issue (none yet) and would be wrong read as "no recovery mint on Base". The supply has been exactly flat only since 2026-10-18 21:12:41 UTC (CCIP BNB-lane traffic moved it ±0.08% between 10-07 and then). #### NEAR BigQuery — logins that do not expire (G7-11, for Jake's decision; nothing changed)

Today: Application Default Credentials from Jake's own Google login (the service-account key route is blocked by his
organisation's `iam.disableServiceAccountKeyCreation`). The refresh token is subject to the Workspace session-control
reauthentication interval, so it lapses and the run skips BigQuery until `gcloud auth application-default login`.

| Option | What it takes | Security trade-off |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Project-level exception + service-account key | An org admin sets `iam.disableServiceAccountKeyCreation` to not enforced on near-data-510309 only; a service account with BigQuery Job User on that project (public datasets need no data role); a JSON key outside the repo; key expiry via `iam.serviceAccountKeyExpiryHours` (e.g. 90 days). | A long-lived secret on disk: whoever copies it can run queries billed to the project until it expires or is deleted. It reaches nothing else (no user access). Recommended if a non-expiring login is wanted. |
| 2. Workspace session control | An admin sets Google Cloud session control for Jake's OU to "never require reauthentication". | Jake's user refresh token never lapses — and it carries his whole user access (other projects too); a stolen token stays valid until revoked. |
| 3. Impersonation (`--impersonate-service-account`) | A service account Jake may impersonate. | Still rides Jake's user token: expires the same way. Narrows what queries can touch, not the login. |
| 4. Run in Google Cloud | A scheduled query (runs as a service account) writing results to a table; the local run reads an exported CSV. | No local credential for the query; more moving parts; the export still needs an access path. |
| 5. Keep ADC (now) | — | Nothing long-lived on disk; the daily run skips BigQuery and says "log in" when it lapses. |

| G8-4a | Subsidy targeting by pool | NOT WIRED (routes probed 2026-10-12). | BUILT: `fetch/subsidy_targeting.py` (tier subsidy_targeting). Per complete epoch, dated at its start: `subsidy_fee_cover_pools` = Σ pool fees ÷ Σ emissions $ over the pools receiving emissions; `subsidy_emissions_to_covered_share` = emissions $ to pools whose own fees ≥ their emissions ÷ all; `subsidy_emissions_usd`. Per-pool table data/subsidy/<project>_<epoch>.csv (blank = unknown, never 0). A pool whose fees cannot be valued is dropped from both sides and NAMED (PARTIAL while ≤5% of emissions, refused above); a pool with unknown emissions refuses the epoch. **Aerodrome** (weekly): the Voter's 2,013 gauged pools, those paid that epoch selected by the gauge's per-epoch rate; RewardsSugar `epochsByAddress` (Multicall3, 20 pools a batch) for each pool's fees paid to veAERO voters that epoch. Emissions are NOT rate × 604,800: the gauge's rate is the epoch's AERO ÷ the time LEFT when distribute() runs, so a gauge paid late reads inflated (USDC/STAR 10-01: 716,751 AERO read vs ~1,305 paid); the rate is re-read 3 h into the epoch and, where it changed, only the AERO paid from the switch block counts (historical state on mainnet.base.org, keyless). Voter "pool" 0x97cd4eb6… (a contract rejecting every call; gauge 0x287c94a1… has no rate) is left out and its vote share logged. AERO priced at the epoch end. **Live: 2026-10-08 — 714 pools, 4,185,455 AERO = $3,783,865 vs fees $1,500,293: cover 0.397, 0.83% of emissions (102 pools) to pools covering them (PARTIAL: 35 pools, 0.04% of emissions unvalued). 2026-10-01 — 622 pools, $3,322,145 vs $1,678,507: cover 0.506, 5.3% (127 pools).** Top 10-08: WETH/USDC $288k vs $112k (0.39); USDC/AERO $274k vs $83k (0.30); WETH/cbBTC $223k vs $101k (0.45); fBOMB/AERO $183k vs $5.7k (0.03); fBOMB/USDbC $102k vs $283. **Pendle** (14-day sPENDLE epochs): no keyless source serves past per-market rates (publicnode refuses archive reads), so each run samples GaugeController `rewardData` for every Ethereum market (data/subsidy/pendle_rates_<epoch>.json) and, when the epoch ends, sums them against Pendle's per-market `poolFeeUsd`; two missed days in a row refuse the epoch. First epoch stored: 2026-10-20..11-03 — only if the tier first runs before 2026-10-22 00:00 UTC, else 11-03..11-17. Ethereum markets only (~two-thirds of 10-06's fees). Votes blank (gauge voting ended under sPENDLE). Preview (not stored; two epochs mixed): ~5,952 PENDLE (~$12.2k) across 36 markets, cover ~4.2. Limits: partner top-ups to an Aerodrome gauge set the same rate and are counted with emissions; Base logs are out of reach keylessly, so exact per-gauge distributions are not read; LP-kept fees of unstaked liquidity are not counted. CP: basis, FEE COVER, share covered. |
| G8-4b | Pendle TVL by maturity | NOT WIRED. | BUILT: `fetch/pendle_maturity.py` (tier pendle_maturity). Pendle's own API: core/v2/markets/all (830 markets, 97 unexpired) and each market's core/v3 daily history; totalTvl (PT held outside the pool included; `tvl` is pool liquidity only — reUSD 2026-10-20: $268M vs $13M) summed by DAYS TO EXPIRY on each day: `tvl_maturity_le_30d_usd`, `_31_90d_`, `_91_180d_`, `_gt_180d_`, and `tvl_maturity_total_usd` (their sum, a check against TVL). An expired market leaves the sum (its frozen totalTvl — $72.3B over 733 expired markets — is no current figure). A day is stored only once every market live that day is read and holds a point; a hole refuses the day for every bucket and the market is named. State checkpointed per market (.cache/pendle_maturity/state.json); computing units paced to the 200-per-minute window, a weekly reserve kept. Live 2026-10-20 (current totalTvl of the 97): ≤30d $0.225B, 31–90d $0.738B, 91–180d $0.046B, >180d $0.007B = **$1.015B**; DefiLlama /tvl/pendle $1.117B (0.909 — what DefiLlama adds was not verified). First read: ~509 markets inside 400 days, ~530 CU of the 200,000 weekly, 4–5 runs; then ~100 calls a day. |
| G8-4b | CCIP token value | NOT WIRED. | BUILT: `fetch/ccip_value.py` (tier ccip_value; history `--seed ccip_value`). The documented keyless API (api.ccip.chain.link v2, spec 2.13.0): /v2/messages walked newest-first by cursor (no date filter; ~2 pages a day), each message's `sourceTokenAmount` (raw, source chain) × 10^-decimals (/v2/tokens directory, cached weekly) × the DefiLlama coins price at 12:00 UTC of the send day (a token DefiLlama does not price on its own chain is priced through a sibling in its CCIP token group). `ccip_token_value_usd` and `ccip_token_transfers` per complete UTC send day; a day is closed only once the walk has passed below it; a day inside the walk with no message is refused (an API gap, not 0). A transfer with no price is left out and COUNTED: the day is stored PARTIAL `[n of m transfers unpriced, x% of messages]`, or refused above max_unpriced_share 10% (live days ran 2.3–3.8%, long-tail tokens). CCIP 2.0 OnRamps reject more than one token a message (`CanOnlySendOneTokenPerMessage`); 1.x lanes are read in detail (recent days all, older days a 10% sample): 0 multi-token messages in 1,145 read. **Live 2026-10-19: $30,468,435 over 773 token-moving messages (of 2,778), 29 transfers unpriced (3.8%) → PARTIAL**; top tokens syrupUSDG (Ethereum) $5.34M, USD1 $3.48M, GHO (Ethereum) $3.40M, GHO (Arbitrum) $3.38M, cbBTC (Monad) $2.71M. Chainlink's fourth business slot. Limits: ~15 chains have no DefiLlama key (e.g. Bittensor, Canton, TON) — unpriced unless a group sibling prices them; the amount is the source amount at send (a message failing on its destination still counts); the API's ~271-day limit caps history at 270 days. |

| G9-B1 | N/A BY DESIGN only from a declared reason | 158 cells N/A BY DESIGN whose own text said gap / no rows / BLOCKED / measuring_point_changed / stale / "inputs are present". | coverage_audit.honest_statuses: the text decides the status — measuring_point_changed → BROKEN; stale → CHECK; BLOCKED for unequal coverage, or a window spanning a base's start → MISSING C with the date it becomes valid; any other BLOCKED / gap / no rows / not tracked / "inputs are present" / "UNCONFIRMED:" → MISSING with its class (missing_class), or N/A BY DESIGN quoting the declaration where an input is declared not applicable. A split whose status is unconfirmed renders "n/a — UNCONFIRMED: no published share…" (was the bare word "unconfirmed", graded VERIFIED (derived)). SPEC §10's labels are "NAMED BLOCKER —" (BLOCKED alone is a refused computation). Fixture: 160 cells reclassified — MISSING C 58, MISSING D 49, MISSING E 4, N/A BY DESIGN with a declared reason 36, VERIFIED 6, VERIFIED (derived) 5, CHECK 2; afterwards 0 N/A BY DESIGN cells match gap / no rows stored / BLOCKED / measuring_point_changed / inputs are present / stale (test). |

Test: equal status for every row with no quarter. |

Its CCIP group spans 68 chains, no DefiLlama price on any of the 63 mapped (current / 09-11 / 09-23), no CoinGecko listing; its verified source is Chainlink's test helper BurnMintERC20WithDrip.sol, `drip(address to) { _mint(to, 1e18); }` — anyone mints it free; 1,391 of 1,398 messages move 1 raw unit, sender = receiver on 2.0 lanes. DECLARED zero-value (spec zero_value_groups, the contract as evidence; valued 0 and counted on the row "[n transfer(s) of a declared zero-value test token valued 0]", never "unpriced"; reversible): the six days fall to 1.75 / 0.86 / 0.86 / 0.72 / 0.68 / 0.91% unpriced. ### G12 — Round G12, "wiring. nothing new is fetched" (Jake, 2026-10-21), old → new

No new source, seed, dry run or API call. Scope: the 16 portfolio projects; Aerodrome is parked (audited, its numbers
unchanged). Evidence: Jake's workbook of run 20261021T152104Z (15:27 UTC). Fixture: the 10-13 dumps at asof 2026-10-13.

THE WIRING RULE. Every Data row with a number in any window is, from the build's OWN dependency graph: (1) SHOWN — a
cell on Master / A1–A4 / CP / Flows / Trajectory reads it (a formula on its Data row, a Flows line naming it, or a
display builder reading it while building a cell — a read made only to style a cell, its confidence fill, flags or
n/a reason, does not count); (2) CROSS-CHECK — a Credibility row reads it (its spec's values, a Credibility formula on
its Data row, or a Credibility formula reading the series); (3) INPUT — a read-time view that built a (1)/(2) series
read it (transitively; the parents named), or a fetcher derives a stored (1)/(2) series from it (config.FETCH_INPUTS,
each module tested to read the parent and write the child); (4) DECLARED NOT SHOWN — config.NOT_SHOWN or a rule
derived from config, with a one-line reason. Anything else is UNWIRED. A series that measures supply, demand or value
capture is SHOWN on its archetype tab; one that measures the same thing as a shown series from another source is a
CROSS-CHECK row (judged where the definitions match, RECORDED — both values and the gap, verdict "N/A (recorded)" —
where they differ by definition or window, the row saying how); one that only builds a shown series is an INPUT.

| Item | Area | Old | New |
|---|---|---|---|
| G12-A1 | Wiring audit | None: Jake's outside audit found ~300 rows reaching no cell. | wiring.py records every read and write of a (project, metric) during the build (TrackingDict over aggregate()'s groups, _LAST_GROUPS and data_by_key; phases "shown" / "cross"; credibility._series notes its reads), attributing each to every named function on the stack down to aggregate(). The audit runs at the end of every build: wiring.json beside the workbook, "WIRING: UNWIRED n" on the Run Log and the scoreboard, `python check_offline_items.py wiring` lists UNWIRED by project with label and latest value (TOKEN_METRICS_WIRING_ALL=1: every class). |
| G12-A2 | Credibility rows on empty cells | 40 rows read a cell left empty by design (CP BUSINESS 3/4, the second market-share basis for a non-chain, A3 protocol staking yield where nothing is staked for yield, A1 NRR for a non-chain): Excel showed "our value 0". | Our value reads "no figure by design", the verdict "N/A (no figure by design)", the note names the cell. Never a measured zero. |

No new source, seed, dry run, BigQuery or extra API call beyond the normal daily run. Evidence: Jake's run 20261022 on
1eb632d (a bad network: 20 sources timed out; 868 calls queued, 4,040 s). UNWIRED 4 (Aerodrome), agreed.

| Item | Area | Old | New |
|---|---|---|---|
| G14-0 | The suite on 1eb632d | 1,462 passed, 1 failed: test_round_g3 test_g3_3 asserted `days_needed(extra_days=600)[0] < 2025-03-01`, a fixed date — false from 2026-10-22, when today − 600 days IS 2025-03-01 (the code is right; the test aged). | The test asserts the claim itself: exactly 600 days back from today, and further than the earliest supply row. |
| G14-1 | A fallback in the primary's series | When the chain tier timed out, CoinGecko's total_supply for Chainlink, Maple, Morpho, Sky, Pendle, Aerodrome, Ether.fi, Aethir and Fluid took 2026-10-22: the on-chain-first rule (fetch._resolve_tier_collisions) only orders the chain row ahead of CoinGecko's when BOTH exist. MEASURING POINT CHANGED (chain:…:token, coingecko), 15 BROKEN cells (CP Total supply ×9, A3 FDV retirement rate ×6), a BASIS violation on Aethir fdv_usd, 12 guard lines. | RULE: a fallback never writes into a series whose declared source is another one. (a) fetch/coingecko writes CoinGecko's total only as total_supply_coingecko for every TOTAL_SUPPLY_ONCHAIN_FIRST project (as already for TOTAL_SUPPLY_NET). (b) fetch._refuse_fallback_into_primary, after the tier collisions, on every run: a row whose measuring point is an EARLIER leg of the series' declared handover than a stored row dated on or before it is not stored (fetch.archive.breaks_handover_order, the rule the archive backfill already obeys) — "FALLBACK REFUSED <project> / <metric> <day> from <source> …; the series keeps its last stored value (<value> on <date>)", a WARN on the run summary. A later leg is never refused (Ethereum's ultrasound → CoinGecko total stays). Replayed over every stored row of the fixture's 91 declared handovers: 0 would have been refused. (c) The stock keeps its last stored value, marked stale with its date: a chain-read stock whose newest row is older than the project's newest price row reads "STALE — last read <date>, not read on <day> (the chain read did not answer); the last stored value stands" on its Data row, AMBER (build_workbook.not_read_since). (d) The 2026-10-22 CoinGecko rows are removed from those nine total_supply series by a declared one-off in the run (config.RUN_CLEANUPS G14-1: project, metric, date and source must all match; each row removed is logged, console and Run Log, "CLEAN-UP G14-1: removed …"; a second run finds nothing). CoinGecko's figure for the day stays in total_supply_coingecko. (e) Ether.fi: CoinGecko's total (965.35M) equals its own circulating, so the Credibility row reads "UNVERIFIABLE — reference unreliable: CoinGecko total = its circulating", never CHECK (credibility.cg_total_is_its_circulating, any project, to 1 token). |
| G14-2 | Flow vs stock across a timed-out stock | GEODNET gross_burn_tokens and Hyperliquid's burn BROKEN "THE FLOWS DO NOT SUM TO THE STOCK'S MOVE" (−105,000; −22,648): the flows were summed to their last row (2026-10-22), the stock's move to ITS last row (10-21, its tier timed out). 9 BROKEN cells, 13 guard lines, 21 GEODNET stored_but_unshown hits. | build_workbook.telescoping reconciles over the dates both hold: it ends at the earlier of the two series' last days; a flow row after it "waits for the <stock> reading it telescopes to" (said on the row), never a residual. A flow that does not add up over the common days is still BROKEN. The "today is not stored" rule is NOT extended to chain-read differenced flows: such a row is the move between two readings (yesterday's and today's), not a partial calendar day — the next reading's difference starts where it ends, so nothing is lost or counted twice, while dropping it would leave today's stock reading with no flow to its move. |
| G14-3 | Ethereum settlement rebuild | settlement_rebuilt_usd 140 days against p2p_transfer_volume_usd's 144; settlement_volume_usd blank 2026-09-20..09-23; settlement_volume_365d_usd and network_reserve_ratio stop at 2026-09-19. | NOT REPRODUCED IN CODE. The rebuild is the inner join of the three legs (a day only where all three are stored; unknown is never 0): a full build with all three legs stored daily gives a rebuild row on every common day (120 of 120 on the fixture with NFT and P2P added), and no read-time view touches the three legs; the store writes every date as YYYY-MM-DD and drops NULL values. On Jake's store one leg must lack those days: DEX and NFT store DefiLlama's whole daily chart each run, so a day DefiLlama's chart lacks is a day the rebuild lacks. Jake's store is not in this environment, so the leg is not named here. `python check_offline_items.py settlement_legs` (store only, no call) prints each leg's rows for 2026-09-15..09-30 (TOKEN_METRICS_FROM / _TO), the build's own read of each leg after every read-time view, and for each day without a rebuild row which leg the store lacks — or "every leg is stored: a READ-TIME drop". Test: three legs stored on a day → a rebuild row that day; a leg lacking a day drops that day only and names the leg. The G12 C5 / G13 3 P2P changes stay: on 10-21 the four P2P days were cached but not stored, on 10-22 they are stored with no "cached day NOT stored" WARN — the only change to that path between the two runs (236c178 → 1eb632d) was G13's, so it is the likely reason they are stored (inferred: the cache itself is not here), and its WARN is the evidence that the P2P read is now not the cause. |

### G16 — Round G16, "two clean-ups. nothing new is fetched" (Jake, 2026-10-23), old → new

No new source, seed, dry run or extra API call. Evidence: Jake's run 2026-10-23 11:47 BST on 19ef3a9 (guard OK; BROKEN 1, a
real Morpho Merkl check, left; UNWIRED 4, Aerodrome) and his `morning_reads` (1,212 flags, 2026-09-22..10-23).

| Item | Area | Old | New |
|---|---|---|---|
| G16-1 | Which latest day is provisional | G15 5: every DefiLlama / CoinGecko daily flow's latest row read before 16:00 UTC the day after. Jake's morning_reads: "the cut-off is too early" — the low reads are SERIES-specific, not time-of-day: Fluid fees_usd and revenue_usd at 11-26% of final at 08-10h UTC on every day listed; Near intents_fees_usd at 4-8% on 6 days (10-13, 15, 17, 18, 19, 20); Near intents_revenue_usd, Near dex_volume_usd (10-06, 14%), Morpho fees_usd (09-21), Hyperliquid revenue_usd (88%) rarer; at least one low read at or after 16:00. | A PER-SERIES LIST (config.provisional_series): a series is listed when it read below 90% of its final value on at least N = 2 distinct days (config.PROVISIONAL_LOW_READ_MIN_DAYS) — counted from the store's Review Queue history by store.low_read_days, the same function morning_reads prints — or when it is DECLARED from Jake's 10-23 morning_reads (config.PROVISIONAL_SERIES_DECLARED: Fluid fees_usd, Fluid revenue_usd, Near intents_fees_usd). The declaration is needed because the Review Queue cannot see a partial day that was never re-read: its "final" is the partial value itself (the fixture's Fluid fees_usd 10-11 is 6,913 against ~110,000 on the days before, read once at 07:08 on 10-12, ratio 1.0 against itself). WHY N = 2: on the 10-13 fixture all six low-reading series read low on exactly one day each (6 of 987 flags: Fluid fees and revenue 1 day each at 08h, Morpho fees 0.02% once, Near dex volume once, Hyperliquid revenue / holders revenue 88% once) — one-offs, each a single event; on Jake's store the repeating series read low on many days (Fluid every day listed, Near intents fees 6). Two distinct days is the smallest count that separates a pattern from an event. A listed series' LATEST row is provisional, whatever the hour, until a later run re-reads it: re-read = at least two runs that read that provider for the project with a success after the day closed, up to the run that wrote the row (store run_log). It is stored, left out of every window and of the Credibility rows, and named on the Data row. Because DefiLlama is read once a day per project (fetch/llama.py's daily mark — not changed: a same-day re-read would be an extra call), on one run a day the next re-read is the next day's run. At fetch time a listed series' yesterday is never the day a change flag judges (the check compares the day before, which this run has re-read). UNLISTED SERIES: NO CUT-OFF. The data: an unlisted series' low reads are single events at 07h-15h on the fixture and at or after 16:00 on Jake's store — no hour separates them, and a late cut-off would hold back every DefiLlama / CoinGecko daily flow's latest day on every run before it to catch about one flag in 160 (6 of 987). `python check_offline_items.py morning_reads` prints the list: each series' low-read days and flags, its hours, and why it is listed (declared / N days on this store) or not. |
| G16-1b | The fixture, 19ef3a9 → G16 | 48 series held provisional (G15's clock). | 2 held: Fluid fees_usd and revenue_usd 10-11 (6,913 and its revenue, read once at 07:08 on 10-12 — the partial day the Review Queue could not see). Near intents_fees_usd 10-12 is released (two runs read it on 10-13, 08:25 and 21:12). The store-derived list is empty on the fixture (every low read is a one-off). 1,151 cells move against 19ef3a9 (Data 401, Coverage 428, Trajectory 251, Credibility 37, Flows 33, Run Log 1), almost all G15's held days returning to their windows (Ethereum Fees Q0 37.00M → 37.39M, as before G15); no status or verdict changes. Undone with it: the two guard-baseline entries G15 rewrote (Aerodrome 87 → 88 days, the a0e74ee baseline again) and the two Aerodrome real-dump figures (today's-price APR 8.44% → 8.52%, price overlap 32 → 33 days): Aerodrome's holders_revenue_usd is not listed. |
| G16-2 | Ether.fi total supply Credibility | G14 1: "UNVERIFIABLE — reference unreliable: CoinGecko total = its circulating" whenever CoinGecko's total equals its own circulating — on Jake's 10-23 store also when CoinGecko's total is 998,535,999, identical to ours (earlier days 965.35M). | The label applies only when ours and CoinGecko's total DISAGREE beyond the row's tolerance (1%) AND CoinGecko's total equals its circulating. When they agree the row is judged as for every other project (PASS). |

No new source or endpoint: rpc.plume.org (item 2) is the RPC every Plume reader already uses. Evidence: Jake's run on
4ebc061 — wiring_complete: 11 open cells outside Aerodrome and World Mobile (Plume 9, Near 1, "(all)" 1); the guard 1
violation; 7,396 formulas, 0 mismatches. Target: wiring_complete prints WIRING COMPLETE and the guard 0 on the next run.

So the rule formed to 10-21; 10-22 took NEAR's own BigQuery figure ("first_party; no lockup read in force"); from 10-23 nothing formed (BigQuery lags) and market cap / circulating_supply_used fell back to CoinGecko's figure, unflagged. CoinGecko never stands in (CIRCULATING_ONCHAIN Near no_coingecko_fallback: another basis). When no formed day is within the carry (3 days) of the latest, circulating_supply_lockup_rule, circulating_supply_used and market_cap_usd read "BLOCKED — NEAR'S LOCKUP RULE CANNOT FORM ON <day>: the last weekly lockup read is <date>, <n> days old … Run python near_lockups_weekly.py". ### D1 — Round D1, "the data phase, round 1" (Jake, 2026-10-26, head 3de3ba9), old → new

Data-phase rules: new reads one item at a time, primary sources first; nothing inferred; a wrong stored number is fixed
or removed before a new one is added. Step 0: the full suite on 3de3ba9 (1,537 passed; guard 0).

Its adapter reads NEAR's Dune dataset and was late: the same afternoon DefiLlama backfilled 10-23..10-25 (103.8M / 95.4M / 93.7M chain totals) and restated the 10-22 fees to 160,221. Same series, same endpoints: no handover. The stale rows are removed by clean-up D1-1 (four exact rows) and the next read re-stores them. From now on a Near day whose breakdown has no NEAR Intents is NOT STORED (the read asks for the breakdown; TRADING_THROUGHPUT Near required_in_breakdown), so the settlement rebuild and NRR refuse it rather than sum it; and an Intents fees day below its revenue day is refused (gross fees are never below the revenue taken from them). Not established: why the adapter lagged, the backfill's exact time, any adapter commit (GitHub API refused), and whether THORSwap's NEAR leg reading 0 on 10-23..10-25 is real. |

A total stores only when every live lockup is read at the pinned block — never a partial total (unchanged). (2) RATE LIMITS: every NEAR RPC call goes through one per-endpoint pacer — fastnear ≥ 0.25 s apart, near.org ≥ 1.0 s apart (the 429s came from near.org); an HTTP 429 or 5xx cools that endpoint for its Retry-After (else 5 s doubling to 60 s) and the call moves to the other endpoint, up to 8 attempts before a lockup counts unread this run; 4 lockups in flight. Neither endpoint publishes a limit, and none was reproduced from here (40 calls/min sequential each, no 429), so the pacer adapts rather than assumes. Expected run: ~1,956 lockups × ~4.5 calls ≈ 8,800 calls; at fastnear's pace (~4/s) plus near.org's (~1/s) ≈ 30–45 min, longer under 429s; progress kept every 25 lockups, so a killed run loses at most 25. If both are right, LOCKED fell 568,724 in ~15 days (~38K a day): a 30-day-old read would overstate LOCKED, and so understate circulating, by up to ~1.1M NEAR (~0.09% of supply); it can never move by more than LOCKED itself (≤ 0.16%). Proposed: a read up to 30 days old forms the figure, AMBER, the read's age on the cell; BLOCK after 30. |
| D2-8a | Silent deaths of the scheduled run (windows/daily_run.ps1, register_task.ps1); the D2-8 run-time estimate | Jake 2026-10-26: the scheduled runs died silently on 10-22 (log ends at adc_check.py), 10-23 (ends after token_metrics starts) and 10-25 (ends 1 minute into the lockup step, after token_metrics finished). The task stopped on battery and was capped at 4 h; Jake has removed both. Two causes in the script: Invoke-Step wrote a step's output to the log only AFTER the step exited, so a killed step left nothing; and a process killed from outside runs no `finally`, so Round G 1.8's "FINAL: stopped early" line is never written. Live smoke of the D2-8 read (2026-10-26, 40 lockups): 259 calls in 97 s (~6.5 calls a lockup, ~2.7 calls/s with 9 throttles) — the D2-8 estimate of ~30–45 min was low. | (1) STREAMED LOG: each step's output goes to the log line by line as it arrives (a FileStream opened for append, shared read/write, flushed every line; PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1), so a killed step keeps everything it printed — the lockup read prints a progress line every 100 lockups with the time taken and an estimate of the time left. (2) A FINAL LINE FOR EVERY STEP, EVEN WHEN KILLED: every step's start and end is recorded in logs\daily_run_state.json (the run's log, its PID and start, the open step and when it started, the steps finished with exit codes). A run that ends normally or by an error marks it final. The NEXT run (or `daily_run.ps1 -Reconcile` by hand) finds a run that is not final and whose PowerShell process is gone, and appends to THAT run's log: `=== <step> : KILLED - no exit code (started …)` and `daily run FINAL: KILLED during <step> - steps that ran: …; not run: …`, marked as reconciled with the time; the new run's log and its console line say so. It never guesses why: the line lists the possible causes (Task Scheduler stop or time limit, battery, sleep, shutdown). (3) register_task.ps1 matches what Jake set: no time limit by default (-LimitHours 0 = none; supersedes D2-8's 8 h), runs and keeps running on battery by default (-StopOnBattery to restore the old behaviour), so re-registering never puts back what he removed. Open risk named: with no limit and MultipleInstances IgnoreNew, a run that HANGS blocks the next morning's run — the reconcile line then appears only when it ends. (4) RUN TIME, corrected from the smoke: ~1,956 × 6.5 ≈ 12,700 calls; at ~2.7 calls/s ≈ 80 min (plus ~7 min for the NearBlocks list without a key, cached a day); longer under 429s. (5) PROPOSAL FOR JAKE (not applied): move the lockup read to its own weekly scheduled task ("Crypto-Tracker NEAR lockups", Sunday 02:15 local, its own log logs\near_lockups_YYYY-MM-DD.log, no overlap with the 06:30 daily run), keeping step 2b in daily_run.ps1 as the fallback only when the last stored read is 8+ days old — so a slow lockup read can never delay or be killed with the daily run, and a failed Sunday read is resumed at its pinned block by Monday's fallback within the 48 h pin. |

ONE READ AT A TIME: near_lockups_weekly takes a lock file (LogCache root near-lockups.lock, holding its PID and start time); a second copy finds a live lock and exits 0 saying so; a lock older than 6 h, or whose process is gone, is taken over. |

Battery behaviour unchanged (runs on battery; -StopOnBattery to restore the old). |

Pendle's 1,410,612 = per-market protocol fee + realized YT fee 1,277,758 + PT-looping service fee 22,105 (spendle/data ptLoopingFees, "already included in fees") + an epoch-level remainder 110,750 that Pendle attributes to no market (its API docs: "Epoch-level PT-looping and unrealized YT fees are not per market") — not broken down further; whether any of it is Boros is NOT ESTABLISHED (per epoch it does not track Boros: 12,442 / 49,291 / 23,553 / 13,972 / 11,492 against Boros 18,212 / 12,086 / 20,693 / 11,529 / 15,925). Airdrops: 0 in all five epochs, so the deduction moves nothing; DefiLlama's "Other Fees" (non-USDT tokens sent to the airdrop distributor 0x3942…A38E from Pendle's treasury and funders) read 0 such transfers on Ethereum over the same days (5 transfers in, none from those senders; the control month 2026-05-20..06-20 finds 2) — Arbitrum not read (its Blockscout answers 403). WHY (DefiLlama's adapter, fees/pendle.ts, read 2026-10-26): it counts the SY and reward tokens RECEIVED by Pendle's treasury per chain, for the markets Pendle lists now, priced by DefiLlama — so tokens it cannot price (Robinhood Chain's tokenised-stock markets, HyperEVM's) read near 0, and treasury sweeps move amounts between epochs. CLASSIFICATION: (1) Boros in ours — a basis error, FIXED (D3-5a); (2) PT-looping fees and (3) the epoch-level remainder — legs Pendle counts that DefiLlama does not read; Pendle publishes them per 14-day epoch only (no daily figure, market-fees keeps 13 epochs), so adding them to the daily revenue_usd would need a spreading rule — NOT ADDED (never filled from inference); DECISION FOR JAKE; (4) Robinhood Chain / HyperEVM / Ethereum timing — DefiLlama's measurement of legs it does read; documented. STORED (first-party, complete epochs, dated at the epoch's start, Pendle's own figures): pendle_epoch_market_revenue_usd (market-fees totals.protocolFeeUsd + totals.realizedYtFeeUsd) and pendle_pt_looping_fees_usd (spendle/data ptLoopingFees; Pendle's API gives 0 for epochs before the fee was tracked, so a 0 is stored only beside a stored ptLoopingVolumes — a fee above 0 is a reading either way: 08-11 11,789 and 08-25 3,111 carry no stored volume). The in_revenue check is UNCHANGED in what it compares and its ±10% tolerance; its working now names the legs per the above. | D4-1 (measured) | NEAR lockups: what the 2026-10-26 read measured | D2/D3 reports estimated the read at ~80 min to ~2.5 h and spoke of a LOCKED "rate". | MEASURED (Jake's log, 2026-10-26/27): the read took 4 h 35 m; 1,955 of 1,956 lockups read; stored 2026-10-26 at block 221,748,149; LOCKED 2,036,357 NEAR (unvested 1,981,418). Pacer: 15,183 calls, 2,733 HTTP 429, 28,620 s cooling. The 2026-10-11 read was 2,058,522, so LOCKED FELL 22,165 NEAR IN 15 DAYS (~1.5K a day) between two complete reads. That is the measured change; NO RATE IS CLAIMED beyond it. |
| D4-1a | The weekly lockup task's time limit; resuming | D3-2: ExecutionTimeLimit 4 h; an unfinished read resumed only when the age gate let the step run (or by hand with --force). | windows/register_lockup_task.ps1 defaults to -LimitHours 8 (Jake: 4 h 35 m measured). ANY run — the weekly task or the daily task's step 2b — resumes an unfinished read at its pinned block while the pin is valid (≤ 48 h, check_offline_items.lockup_resumable: a cache with a pin and no `complete` mark), whatever the age gate says. A complete read is marked `complete` and is never resumed. |
| D4-1b | The lockup read's ETA | Calls per lockup × the raw pacing interval (no throttling). | From THROTTLED THROUGHPUT: calls per second over a whole read, cooling included. Default 15,183 calls / 16,500 s = 0.92 calls/s (Jake's read); each read of 200+ lockups saves its own figure (LogCache near-lockups-throughput.json) for the next ETA. ETA = lockups × calls per lockup on the route (getters 7.8, measured on Jake's read: 15,183 / 1,955; state 2.4) ÷ throughput. The getters route at the default reproduces 4.6 h. |
| D4-1c | A faster PRIMARY route for the lockup read | Four to six getter calls per lockup (get_balance, get_locked_amount, get_owners_balance, get_vesting_information [, get_unvested_amount]) on the public RPCs, each a contract execution. | The STATE ROUTE (NEAR_LOCKUP_ROUTE = "state"): per lockup, view_account (balance, code hash) + view_state (the contract's Borsh state), then near_locked_getter replicates getters.rs of core-contracts@1b0436c9 exactly (get_balance = account balance + the known staking deposit; locked from the state's own transfers info and lockup / release / vesting; owners = balance − locked, floored at 0). Same primary data (the chain's own state at the pinned block), no aggregate. Applies only to the code hashes validated against the getters (NEAR_LOCKUP_STATE_CODES); any other code, or a state whose tail does not parse, goes to the getters. EVERY 20th LOCKUP (by account-name hash) IS READ BOTH WAYS; ONE MISMATCH REFUTES THE ROUTE FOR THE WHOLE READ: every state-route result is dropped, the read resumes on the getters, and the route is recorded as refuted. VALIDATION (2026-10-27, block 221,804,206): view_account of all 1,956 lockups (1,704 s here, 222 of 2,179 calls throttled) gave six code hashes: 3kVY… 1,725 (the buggy code), 4Pfw… 125, 3skH… 101, Cw7b… 3, DiC9… 1 (buggy), ETTv… 1. 160 lockups sampled (seed 7) and read both ways: fixed code 99 — balance, locked, owners 99/99 and unvested 30/30 equal to the yoctoNEAR; buggy code 60 — balance 60/60 and unvested 28/28 equal (locked and owners are the lakehouse recomputation on both routes, D2 decision 8; the raw buggy get_owners_balance differed on 9, as it does on the getters route). The 160th was the ETTv… account (D4-1d). NEAR_LOCKUP_STATE_CODES = 3kVY…, 4Pfw…, 3skH…, Cw7b… (1,954 of 1,956 lockups); DiC9… (not sampled) and ETTv… go to the getters. ETA at Jake's throughput: 1,956 × 2.4 / 0.92 ≈ 1.4 h against 4.6 h — an ESTIMATE until the next full read saves its own throughput; the state route has not yet run a full read end to end. |
| D4-1d | The lockup whose getter fails | The read logged "1,955 of 1,956"; the missing lockup was not named. | NAMED (probe 2026-10-27, block 221,804,206): 3e0161be1eac063e075d9511fc709297493e0810.lockup.near — the only one of the 1,956 whose getters fail: every getter answers "wasm execution failed … MethodResolveError(MethodNotFound)"; its code (ETTv…, on no other lockup) is not the lockup contract and its state is 25 bytes, not a lockup layout. Its balance: 32.76 NEAR (view_account amount 32,762,576,227,750,887,996,768,528 yocto; its own validator stake 0). THE BOUND ON THE OMISSION: LOCKED can be off by at most 32.76 NEAR (0.0016% of the 2,036,357 LOCKED) — counting only what the account holds; a stake it delegated to a pool would be recorded in a lockup's state, and it has none, so none is known. It is left out, as before. Every run names each lockup left out with its balance (view_account) and states "LOCKED can be off by at most that". |
| D4-2a | Alchemy getLogs | The resilient walk sent Alchemy ranges up to the general chunk (2,000 blocks); Alchemy's free plan rejects more than 10 blocks per eth_getLogs, so each call failed and the walk halved down, one wasted call at a time, inside the tier's budget. | ALCHEMY_GETLOGS_MAX_RANGE = 10 (env ALCHEMY_GETLOGS_MAX_RANGE overrides it for a paid plan). The walk never sends Alchemy a range above it: a chunk to an Alchemy URL is capped at the plan's range, and a span needing more than 20 calls at that range (SMALL_SPAN_CALLS) is not sent to Alchemy at all — it goes to the next endpoint (etherscan / Blockscout / public RPC); scan_logs leads with Alchemy only when it would not be refused. |

Measured live 2026-10-27: all 216 roots in 234 s — cannot fit 120 s. | A CACHE (LogCache merkl-allocations.json): a campaign that ENDED at least 30 days before its read is FINAL and not re-read; each cached entry is re-verified every 28 + (id mod 14) days, so re-reads spread over the month, and a change found is logged. Running and recently ended campaigns are read every run (measured 2026-10-27: 208 cached, 8 re-read, 26 s with the campaign lists). The reader stops itself at 100 s (READ_BUDGET_S, inside the tier's 120 s), keeps every campaign read in the cache, and stores NOTHING that run (a sum missing campaigns would understate); the next run continues. One-off first fill outside the run: `python -m fetch.merkl_campaigns --fill` (216 roots in 234 s measured; stores nothing). Without it the daily run fills the cache 100 s at a time — three runs —. Rows now carry source merkl_campaigns:allocated. A WRONG STORED SERIES, REMOVED FIRST: the store keys a row by (date, project, metric), so allocated rows overwrite schedule rows only on the days they cover — a schedule row after the allocated series' end (a running campaign computed by Merkl only to an earlier day) would stay, on the old basis. CLEAN-UP D4-2b (once, the D3-5 `once` selector) removes every row of merkl_campaigns_non_ethereum_tokens (Morpho) and merkl_campaigns_plume_tokens (Plume) stored under source "merkl_campaigns" (the schedule basis, and on Jake's store nothing else: his D2 run's Merkl read timed out) and clears the series' daily read mark, so the same run re-reads both in full on the allocated basis; if that read does not complete, the series stays empty (MISSING) until it does — never the schedule figure. |
| D4-2c | Code-caused vs network-caused timeouts | "TIER TIMED OUT after Ns" with no cause; five sources timing out tripped NETWORK-WIDE TROUBLE. | Each TIER TIMED OUT line ends "CAUSE: <cause> — <evidence>" from the tier's own calls (fetch.timeout_cause; Http stats now count HTTP 429 and network errors; RPC tiers use latency_summary): NETWORK (≥3 timeouts / connection errors / 5xx, or ≥3 calls of 10 s+, or a tenth of calls failed); THROTTLED (≥3 HTTP 429 and a quarter of the budget waiting); CODE (every call answered at its usual speed and the budget still ran out); UNCLASSIFIED otherwise. CODE and THROTTLED timeouts are listed under "TIMED OUT, NOT THE NETWORK" and never count towards NETWORK-WIDE TROUBLE. A build to any other path (the guard, sanity, cross_tab, the tests) writes the full workbook alone, as before. |
| P1-2 | Reader cell rules | Text and numbers mixed in value cells; headers up to ~200 characters with internal codes (Q0, G/D rounds, config names); trend columns as strings ("↑ · +4% · …"). | A value cell holds a NUMBER (a formula on `_data`), or one short code — `n/a` (no value; the reason), `blocked` (a rule refused it), `not wired` (no route yet), `by design` (chased and closed, or suppressed by rule) — in the code's colour, the full reason in the cell comment. Headers ≤ 35 characters, plain English; the full definition in the header's comment and on Definitions. No internal codes: "Q0" reads "last 90 days", no round numbers, no config names. Formats: $ as $m / $bn, ratios 0.0x, shares %. Confidence stays the cell's fill (GREEN / AMBER / RED, as on the audit cell it comes from). Each trend becomes numbers: quarter values with an arrow icon set (▲ up, ► flat within ±1%, ▼ down). A descriptive column (status, destination) is text and sits in the collapsed group. |
| P1-3 | Reader tab order | — | About, Summary, Business, A1, A2, A3, A4, Comparison, Project View, Charts, Definitions (Stage 1: About, Summary, A3, Definitions). |

Columns: project, token, archetype(s), market cap, FDV, revenue annualised (the trailing 90 days' revenue × 365 ÷ the days they cover — the series Master's "Revenue Q0" reads, config.revenue_headline_metric), revenue growth (selected period), archetype key, rank, holder value yield (CP), total dilution % of tradeable float (CP) and in tokens a year (that % × tradeable float), net holder yield (CP), net supply change % of circulating a year (Master) and tokens over the last 90 days (Master), payout ratio (CP: credited holder value ÷ revenue), price change (selected period), confidence, what changed. |
| P1-5 | The period selector | — | Summary cell B2: 1D / 7D / 30D / 90D / 6M / 1Y / 3Y (default 90D; 6M = 182 days, 1Y = 365, 3Y = 1,095). Two columns follow it, each precomputed in Python for all seven periods (on `_data`) and picked by INDEX / MATCH: REVENUE GROWTH over P = revenue summed over the P days to the series' last day before the as-of date ÷ revenue summed over the P days before them − 1 (every day of both windows held; the windows end where the series ends, since revenue providers lag a day or two — the comment names both windows); PRICE CHANGE over P = price on the last day held ÷ price exactly P days before − 1. Where the history is shorter the cell shows "—" with the reason in its comment (the first day held). Designed for a later custom start date (one more `_data` column pair); not built. |
| P1-6 | Archetype keys (higher = better) | — | A1: SETTLEMENT VOLUME PER CIRCULATING TOKEN, 1-YEAR CHANGE = (trailing-365-day settlement volume ÷ circulating supply, now) ÷ (the same a year earlier) − 1 (Artemis DEX + NFT + P2P, the series A1's NRR reads; needs 730 days). A2: CUSTOMER REVENUE ÷ EMISSIONS $ (x) — A2's existing column. A3: RETIRED BUYBACK ÷ EMISSIONS (x), unlocks EXCLUDED, at the emission rate IN FORCE (config.CURRENT_EMISSION_RATE where declared — Sky — else the last 90 days' measured rate, which is the rate in force) — a new audit A3 column built like "RETIREMENT ÷ DILUTION" with the unlock leg dropped; the unlock-inclusive figure (RETIREMENT ÷ DILUTION, at the rate in force) beside it. A4: BURN ÷ ISSUANCE (x) — A4's existing crossover column, on its issuance basis (pre-minted supply: burn ÷ pool release); no A4 issuance has a declared rate in force, so the last 90 days' measured rate is the rate in force. NO MEASURED KEY → ranked LAST in its group (by name), the reason in the rank cell's comment. |

WHAT CHANGED = the Summary figures that moved more than 10% since the previous run's snapshot (export_snapshot, the same keys the continuity guard reads), largest first, at most three; "no figure moved more than 10% since <date>"; "no previous run to compare" when there is none. |

Every "Detail —" column goes to Project View (Stage 2; on the audit A3 until then); Notes and Data flags go into the project cell's comment. |
